PHASE 11 — MUTATION EVIDENCE, SIXTH CHALLENGE ROUND
===================================================
Twelve mutations, each applied at STEP 5c on its OWN full rebuild from zero,
BEFORE any proof ran. Every count names its pass (P3-01 of round 5).

A METHOD FAILURE OF MINE, FIRST, because it is the reason this file was
re-measured: I launched a second mutation loop while the first was still
running, so TWO harness runs were rebuilding the same scratch cluster at the
same time. The contaminated segments (M16, M17 and the first M18) were
DISCARDED, not reported, and M16 through M19 were re-run strictly serially.
"An authoritative run owns the box" is a rule this container already had, and I
broke it. The contaminated log is kept out of the repository; what follows is
the serial measurement.

THE ROUND-6 MUTATIONS, AND WHAT EACH ONE ESTABLISHES
----------------------------------------------------
M11 (rewritten). The identity function stops distinguishing sessions, so
  unanimity is trivially satisfied. 12 red on pass 1, and they are the arm's
  whole dependency set: the round-5 challenger's exploit and its rollback
  assertion, the DELETE sibling and its rollback assertion, PA6-05, PA6-06,
  PA6-09 and its assertion, PA6-L5, PA6-L6, PA6-L7, and the RLS-scoping
  control. It is applied by replacing one small function rather than by
  pasting a copy of the big one: a pasted copy drifts, and would keep reding
  after the real function changed while the log still said the guard was tested.

M14. Restores the two privileges whose ABSENCE makes a recorded identity
  unforgeable and unremovable (INSERT on recorded_identity, and UPDATE/DELETE
  on the ledger). Exactly 3 red on both passes -- PA6-L1, PA6-L3, PA6-L4 -- so
  those three absences are load-bearing and a suite notices when they are not.
  What it does NOT show, stated so no reader infers it: that the grant reopens
  the fail-open. With DELETE granted an attacker could delete the honest rows
  and leave a forged one, and no case performs that sequence, because on the
  unmutated schema the DELETE is refused and the case would be unwritable.

M15 / M16 / M17 / M18 / M19 are the PLANT-DEFENCE pair-drop, and the result is
  ASYMMETRIC. Reported in full because two of my own predictions were wrong:

  * M15 (drop the write policy outright) does NOT make the plant possible. On a
    table with ENABLE + FORCE row-level security, dropping the only INSERT
    policy means NO insert is permitted at all: the recorder cannot write, and
    9 cases red -- the generated sweep's INSERT legs, PA6-05/06/09, PA6-L5,
    PA6-L8 and the privilege-withdrawal case, i.e. every case that needs a
    write to go through. Fail-closed and broken, not a hole. The wrong
    prediction is kept visible in the mutation file: a corrected expectation is
    evidence, a deleted one is not.
  * M16 (drop the xmin filter alone) reds NOTHING, on either pass, and its
    EXIT_SCRIPT is 0 -- a mutation run that looks exactly like a clean run.
    Only the digest says anything happened. That is why the hook digests the
    schema (round 4, P2-02) and why M10 exists.
  * M17 (both) behaves as M15: 9 red, for M15's reason.
  * M18 (REPLACE the policy with a permissive one) is the plant defence's real
    red proof: exactly 1 red on both passes, PA6-L2, and nothing else moves.
  * M19 (permissive policy AND no xmin filter) also reds exactly PA6-L2 and
    nothing else. So removing the xmin filter on top of the permissive policy
    changes no verdict this suite can observe.

  CONCLUSION, named as a residual rather than counted as a layer: the RLS write
  policy is the plant defence this suite MEASURES. The xmin filter is defence
  in depth whose effect is UNMEASURED -- no case can reach it, because the
  policy makes the planted state unreachable, and a plant aimed at ANOTHER
  session's future transaction id cannot be written as a test, since
  transaction ids are assigned by the server and not chosen by the test.
  TWO GREEN LAYERS ARE NOT TWO TESTED LAYERS.

  And what no mutation here can show, stated plainly: that a planted row makes
  the law fail OPEN. It cannot, because unanimity makes a disagreeing row a
  refusal. The exposure a plant creates is a DENIAL -- someone else's lawful
  transaction refusing -- which is why the policy is described as protecting
  availability rather than correctness.

M7 / M8 / M9 are the original pair-drop, re-measured because this round added
  13 cases: a count carried forward from the previous round would be a figure
  about a different suite. M9's Pack A race fixture is still unbuildable
  (EXIT=2) for the documented reason, and M7/M8 leave all 13 races passing.

NOT EVIDENCE: no CI, no timing claim, no challenger signature. The repository's
own npm gates are a separate measurement and are reported in
P11-LIVE-DDL-PROOF.md 14.3, never in the same sentence as an EXIT_* above.

=== 5c. MUTATION APPLIED: /home/user/daftar/prep/phase11/harness/mutations/M11-defeat-the-write-identity-comparison.sql (this run is NOT a clean-tree proof run) ===
  | -- M11 (round 5, rewritten for the round-6 store). The red proof of the
  | -- write-identity arm itself.
  | --
  | -- Round 5's version replaced catalog_grid_closed_check with a pasted copy of
  | -- its body minus the identity test. That is a second implementation of a
  | -- function this phase keeps changing, and a pasted copy drifts: it would keep
  | -- reding long after the real function moved on, and the log would still say
  | -- the guard was tested. So the mutation now defeats the arm at its narrowest
  | -- point instead -- the identity function stops distinguishing sessions:
  | -- every identity becomes equal, unanimity is trivially satisfied, and the
  | -- comparison with the identity at COMMIT always holds.
  | --
  | -- Expected: every case that rests on the arm goes red by name, including the
  | -- round-5 challenger's exploit, the DELETE sibling, and the round-6 forgeries
  | -- PA6-05, PA6-06 and PA6-09, whose refusal comes from this comparison and not
  | -- from the GUC they forge. The RLS-scoping control stays green: the read is
  | -- scoped, not broken, and this mutation does not touch row-level security.
  | --
  | -- Note for a reader: a constant identity is NOT the same mutation as dropping
  | -- the ledger. The ledger still records a row per statement; what is gone is
  | -- its ability to tell two sessions apart. That is the arm being tested.
  | CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION catalog_grid_ctx_identity()
  | RETURNS TEXT
  | LANGUAGE sql STABLE SECURITY INVOKER
  | SET search_path = pg_catalog, public, pg_temp
  | AS $fn$
  |   SELECT 'one-identity-for-every-session'::text;
  | $fn$;
MUTATION_DDL_DIGEST_BEFORE: 678de1679af747eeb4c7e85e73dbeacf
MUTATION_DDL_DIGEST_AFTER : a6613800b4d0e9a84979b96a6ead72c9

=== 5c. MUTATION APPLIED: /home/user/daftar/prep/phase11/harness/mutations/M14-restore-the-ledgers-forgeability.sql (this run is NOT a clean-tree proof run) ===
  | -- M14 (round 6). The ledger that replaced the forgeable GUC is only as strong
  | -- as three ABSENCES: no role may write `recorded_identity`, no role holding
  | -- INSERT holds UPDATE or DELETE, and RLS pins every row to its own
  | -- transaction. This mutation restores the first two, which is exactly what a
  | -- careless later migration or a "just give the app role what it needs" grant
  | -- would do, and it is the red proof that those absences are load-bearing
  | -- rather than decoration.
  | --
  | -- Expected: the three cases that measure them by name go red --
  | --   PA6-L1 (supplying recorded_identity is permission denied)
  | --   PA6-L3 (UPDATE on the ledger is permission denied)
  | --   PA6-L4 (DELETE on the ledger is permission denied)
  | -- and nothing else changes, because the challenger's own forgeries
  | -- (PA6-05/06/09) do not use these privileges: they forge the GUC, which the
  | -- check no longer reads at all.
  | --
  | -- What this mutation CANNOT show, stated so no reader infers it: that the
  | -- grant re-opens the fail-open. With DELETE granted an attacker could delete
  | -- the honest rows and leave a forged one, and unanimity would then pass -- but
  | -- no case in the suite performs that sequence, because on the unmutated
  | -- schema the DELETE is refused and the case would be unwritable. The claim
  | -- here is the narrow one the measurement supports: these three privileges are
  | -- absent, and a suite notices when they are not.
  | --
  | -- The end-state assertion that checks the same three absences has ALREADY run
  | -- by the time step 5c applies this file (the documented ordering limit), so it
  | -- is not the instrument that reds here.
  | GRANT UPDATE, DELETE ON catalog_grid_ctx_ledger TO daftar_app;
  | GRANT INSERT (recorded_identity) ON catalog_grid_ctx_ledger TO daftar_app;
MUTATION_DDL_DIGEST_BEFORE: 678de1679af747eeb4c7e85e73dbeacf
MUTATION_DDL_DIGEST_AFTER : 380ef55f567c467ac71b332597208eb1

  | -- another transaction: the RLS `WITH CHECK (txid = pg_current_xact_id())` that
  | -- makes the plant impossible rather than merely harmless.
  | --
  | -- Expected: PA6-L2 goes red -- the plant for a future transaction id, which
  | -- this policy refuses by name with `new row violates row-level security
  | -- policy`, now succeeds. Nothing else changes: unanimity means a planted row
  | -- can only cause a refusal, never a pass, so what this policy protects is
  | -- AVAILABILITY (a stranger must not be able to make a later lawful
  | -- transaction refuse), and the suite's other cases do not depend on it.
  | --
  | -- Pair-dropped with M16 and M17, which is the P5 R9 lesson: a one-at-a-time
  | -- sweep cannot see two defences that mask each other.
  | DROP POLICY catalog_grid_ctx_ledger_own_txid_insert ON catalog_grid_ctx_ledger;

=== 5c. MUTATION APPLIED: /home/user/daftar/prep/phase11/harness/mutations/M16-drop-the-xmin-filter.sql (this run is NOT a clean-tree proof run) ===
  | -- M16 (round 6). The SECOND defence against a planted row: the deferred check
  | -- counts only ledger rows whose system column `xmin` equals this
  | -- transaction's id, so a row that exists for this `txid` without having been
  | -- written by this transaction is ignored rather than counted.
  | --
  | -- The mutation is applied by TEXT SURGERY on the live function body rather
  | -- than by pasting a copy of it. A pasted copy is a second implementation that
  | -- drifts: it would keep reding long after the real function changed, and the
  | -- log would still say the guard was tested. This way the mutation refuses
  | -- outright if the line it targets is not in the body it finds.
  | --
  | -- Expected, and this is the honest part: **nothing reds.** The RLS write
  | -- policy of M15 makes the plant impossible in the first place, so no case in
  | -- the suite can reach a state where the `xmin` filter is the arm that
  | -- answers. That makes it defence in depth whose effect is UNMEASURED, not a
  | -- guard this suite proves, and it is named as a residual in
  | -- P11-LIVE-DDL-PROOF.md 14.5 rather than counted as a layer that was tested.
  | -- A mutation that reds nothing is information, which is why the hook digests
  | -- the schema and refuses a mutation that changed nothing at all (M10): this
  | -- one DOES change the schema, and reds no case.
  | DO $m16$
  | DECLARE
  |   v_def  TEXT;
  |   v_new  TEXT;
  |   v_line TEXT := '     AND l.xmin = pg_current_xact_id()::xid';
  | BEGIN
  |   SELECT pg_get_functiondef(p.oid) INTO v_def
  |     FROM pg_proc p
  |    WHERE p.pronamespace = 'public'::regnamespace
  |      AND p.proname = 'catalog_grid_closed_check';
  |   IF v_def IS NULL THEN
  |     RAISE EXCEPTION 'M16: catalog_grid_closed_check is not in public; the mutation has no subject';
  |   END IF;
  |   IF position(v_line in v_def) = 0 THEN
  |     RAISE EXCEPTION 'M16: the xmin filter is not in the live body, so this mutation would silently test nothing';
  |   END IF;
  |   v_new := replace(v_def, v_line, '');
  |   EXECUTE v_new;
  |   -- And the mutation proves it mutated, by name, in the direction it claims.
  |   SELECT pg_get_functiondef(p.oid) INTO v_def
  |     FROM pg_proc p
  |    WHERE p.pronamespace = 'public'::regnamespace
  |      AND p.proname = 'catalog_grid_closed_check';
  |   IF position(v_line in v_def) <> 0 THEN
  |     RAISE EXCEPTION 'M16: the replacement did not take; the xmin filter is still in the live body';
  |   END IF;
  | END;
  | $m16$;
MUTATION_DDL_DIGEST_BEFORE: 678de1679af747eeb4c7e85e73dbeacf
MUTATION_DDL_DIGEST_AFTER : 93d04352a5681c713a9240a174e10225

=== 5c. MUTATION APPLIED: /home/user/daftar/prep/phase11/harness/mutations/M17-drop-both-plant-defences.sql (this run is NOT a clean-tree proof run) ===
  | -- M17 (round 6). The pair of M15 and M16 dropped TOGETHER, which is the whole
  | -- reason this phase pair-drops: a one-at-a-time sweep cannot see two defences
  | -- that mask each other (the P5 R9 lesson).
  | --
  | -- Expected: PA6-L2 goes red, as under M15 alone, and nothing more. That result
  | -- is the honest finding of the pair-drop and is reported as such: the two
  | -- defences are NOT symmetric. The RLS write policy is the one the suite
  | -- measures; the `xmin` filter has no case that can reach it, because the
  | -- policy makes the state it defends against unreachable. The pair-drop is
  | -- what makes that visible instead of letting two green layers imply two
  | -- tested layers.
  | DROP POLICY catalog_grid_ctx_ledger_own_txid_insert ON catalog_grid_ctx_ledger;
  | DO $m17$
  | DECLARE
  |   v_def  TEXT;
  |   v_line TEXT := '     AND l.xmin = pg_current_xact_id()::xid';
  | BEGIN
  |   SELECT pg_get_functiondef(p.oid) INTO v_def
  |     FROM pg_proc p
  |    WHERE p.pronamespace = 'public'::regnamespace
  |      AND p.proname = 'catalog_grid_closed_check';
  |   IF position(v_line in v_def) = 0 THEN
  |     RAISE EXCEPTION 'M17: the xmin filter is not in the live body, so this mutation would silently test nothing';
  |   END IF;
  |   EXECUTE replace(v_def, v_line, '');
  | END;
  | $m17$;

=== 5c. MUTATION APPLIED: /home/user/daftar/prep/phase11/harness/mutations/M18-make-the-write-policy-permissive.sql (this run is NOT a clean-tree proof run) ===
  | -- M18 (round 6). THE red proof of the plant defence, and it exists because
  | -- M15's first stated expectation was WRONG and the measurement said so.
  | --
  | -- M15 drops the own-transaction write policy outright. I predicted that the
  | -- plant (PA6-L2) would then succeed and that case would red. It does not: on a
  | -- table with ENABLE + FORCE row-level security, dropping the only INSERT
  | -- policy means NO insert is permitted at all, so the recorder itself can no
  | -- longer write and every lawful write breaks. That is fail-closed and broken,
  | -- not a hole, and M15's file now says so.
  | --
  | -- To actually reach the planted state the policy must be REPLACED by a
  | -- permissive one rather than removed. That is this file.
  | --
  | -- Expected: PA6-L2 goes red -- the row planted for a future transaction id is
  | -- accepted where the real policy refuses it by name. Nothing else should
  | -- change: unanimity means a planted row can only cause a refusal, never a
  | -- pass, so what the policy protects is AVAILABILITY. M19 then removes the
  | -- `xmin` filter as well, which is the only arrangement in which a planted row
  | -- is actually counted.
  | DROP POLICY catalog_grid_ctx_ledger_own_txid_insert ON catalog_grid_ctx_ledger;
  | CREATE POLICY catalog_grid_ctx_ledger_own_txid_insert ON catalog_grid_ctx_ledger
  |   FOR INSERT WITH CHECK (true);
MUTATION_DDL_DIGEST_BEFORE: 678de1679af747eeb4c7e85e73dbeacf
MUTATION_DDL_DIGEST_AFTER : c39ec0602d26a0d43b04565e18f10159

=== 5c. MUTATION APPLIED: /home/user/daftar/prep/phase11/harness/mutations/M19-permissive-write-policy-and-no-xmin-filter.sql (this run is NOT a clean-tree proof run) ===
  | -- M19 (round 6). The PAIR of M18 and M16 together: the write policy made
  | -- permissive AND the `xmin` filter removed. This is the only arrangement in
  | -- which a row planted for another transaction is actually COUNTED by the
  | -- deferred check, and it is therefore the only arrangement in which the
  | -- `xmin` filter's effect is observable at all.
  | --
  | -- Expected: PA6-L2 goes red, as under M18. Whether anything else moves is the
  | -- measurement this file exists to take, and the answer is written into
  | -- P11-LIVE-DDL-PROOF.md 14.4 rather than predicted here -- a mutation file
  | -- that states its result in advance and is not re-read is how this phase
  | -- shipped a false expectation in M13 and again in M15.
  | --
  | -- What NO mutation in this set can show, stated plainly: that a planted row
  | -- turns the law into a fail-OPEN. It cannot, because unanimity makes a
  | -- disagreeing row a refusal. The exposure a plant creates is a denial --
  | -- someone else's lawful transaction refusing -- and this suite has no case that
  | -- plants a row for a transaction id another session will later use, because
  | -- transaction ids are assigned by the server and not chosen by the test.
  | DROP POLICY catalog_grid_ctx_ledger_own_txid_insert ON catalog_grid_ctx_ledger;
  | CREATE POLICY catalog_grid_ctx_ledger_own_txid_insert ON catalog_grid_ctx_ledger
  |   FOR INSERT WITH CHECK (true);
  | DO $m19$
  | DECLARE
  |   v_def  TEXT;
  |   v_line TEXT := '     AND l.xmin = pg_current_xact_id()::xid';
  | BEGIN
  |   SELECT pg_get_functiondef(p.oid) INTO v_def
  |     FROM pg_proc p
  |    WHERE p.pronamespace = 'public'::regnamespace
  |      AND p.proname = 'catalog_grid_closed_check';
  |   IF position(v_line in v_def) = 0 THEN
  |     RAISE EXCEPTION 'M19: the xmin filter is not in the live body, so this mutation would silently test nothing';
  |   END IF;
  |   EXECUTE replace(v_def, v_line, '');
  | END;
  | $m19$;
MUTATION_DDL_DIGEST_BEFORE: 678de1679af747eeb4c7e85e73dbeacf
MUTATION_DDL_DIGEST_AFTER : a10270446d962b23c8e33d4526be6878
